data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1063604349">.mw-parser-output .navbar{display:inline;font-size:88%;font-weight:normal}.mw-parser-output .navbar-collapse{float:left;text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .navbar-boxtext{word-spacing:0}.mw-parser-output .navbar ul{display:inline-block;white-space:nowrap;line-height:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-mini{font-size:114%;margin:0 4em}</style><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a href="/wiki/Template:Administrative_divisions_of_France" <p><b>Clipperton Island</b> (French: <i lang="fr">Île de Clipperton</i> or <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Île de la Passion</i></span>; Spanish: <i lang="es">Isla de la Pasión</i>) is an uninhabited, 6&#160;<a href="/wiki/Square_kilometre" title="Square kilometre">km<sup>2</sup></a> (2.3&#160;<a href="/wiki/Square_mile" title="Square mile">sq&#160;mi</a>) coral <a href="/wiki/Atoll" title="Atoll">atoll</a> in the eastern <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Ocean" title="Pacific Ocean">Pacific Ocean</a>. It is 10,677&#160;<a href="/wiki/Kilometre" title="Kilometre">km</a> (6,634&#160;<a href="/wiki/Mile" title="Mile">mi</a>) from <a href="/wiki/Paris" title="Paris">Paris</a>, <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a>, 5,400&#160;<a href="/wiki/Kilometre" title="Kilometre">km</a> (2,900&#160;<a href="/wiki/Nautical_mile" title="Nautical mile">nmi</a>) from <a href="/wiki/Papeete" title="Papeete">Papeete</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tahiti" title="Tahiti">Tahiti</a>, and 1,080&#160;<a href="/wiki/Kilometre" title="Kilometre">km</a> (580&#160;<a href="/wiki/Nautical_mile" title="Nautical mile">nmi</a>) from <a href="/wiki/Mexico" title="Mexico">Mexico</a>. It is an <a href="/wiki/Overseas_France" title="Overseas France">overseas state private property of France</a> under direct authority of the <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_the_Overseas_(France)" class="mw-redirect" title="Minister of the Overseas (France)">Minister of the Overseas</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> In the past, Clipperton Island was the subject of a sovereignty dispute in particular between <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a> and <a href="/wiki/Mexico" title="Mexico">Mexico</a>, which was finally settled through <a href="/wiki/Arbitration" title="Arbitration">arbitration</a> in 1931; the <i>Clipperton Island Case</i> remains widely studied in <a href="/wiki/International_law" title="International law">international law</a> textbooks.<sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Localisation_de_l%27ile_de_Clipperton.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Localisation_de_l%27ile_de_Clipperton.png/220px-Localisation_de_l%27ile_de_Clipperton.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Localisation_de_l%27ile_de_Clipperton.png/330px-Localisation_de_l%27ile_de_Clipperton.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Localisation_de_l%27ile_de_Clipperton.png/440px-Localisation_de_l%27ile_de_Clipperton.png 2x" data-file-width="783" data-file-height="551" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Localisation_de_l%27ile_de_Clipperton.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Location of Clipperton Island</div></div></div>
<table class="wikitable floatright">
<caption>Clipperton Island
</caption>
<tbody><tr>
<th></th>
<th>area
</th></tr>
<tr>
<td>land</td>
<td>2&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (0.77&#160;sq&#160;mi)
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td>land + lagoon</td>
<td>6&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (2.3&#160;sq&#160;mi)
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td><a href="/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone" title="Exclusive economic zone">EEZ</a></td>
<td>431,273&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (166,515&#160;sq&#160;mi)
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>The atoll is 1,080&#160;<a href="/wiki/Kilometre" title="Kilometre">km</a> (583&#160;<a href="/wiki/Nautical_mile" title="Nautical mile">nmi</a>) south-west of Mexico, 2,424&#160;<a href="/wiki/Kilometre" title="Kilometre">km</a> (1,309&#160;<a href="/wiki/Nautical_mile" title="Nautical mile">nmi</a>) west of <a href="/wiki/Nicaragua" title="Nicaragua">Nicaragua</a>, 2,545&#160;<a href="/wiki/Kilometre" title="Kilometre">km</a> (1,374&#160;<a href="/wiki/Nautical_mile" title="Nautical mile">nmi</a>) west of <a href="/wiki/Costa_Rica" title="Costa Rica">Costa Rica</a> and 2,260&#160;<a href="/wiki/Kilometre" title="Kilometre">km</a> (1,220&#160;<a href="/wiki/Nautical_mile" title="Nautical mile">nmi</a>) north-west of the <a href="/wiki/Gal%C3%A1pagos_Islands" title="Galápagos Islands">Galápagos Islands</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ecuador" title="Ecuador">Ecuador</a>, at <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1073938472"/><span class="plainlinks nourlexpansion"><a class="external text" href="//geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Clipperton_Island&amp;params=10_18_N_109_13_W_type:isle_region:FR-CP&amp;title=Clipperton+Island"><span class="geo-default"><span class="geo-dms" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location"><span class="latitude">10°18′N</span> <span class="longitude">109°13′W</span></span></span><span class="geo-multi-punct">&#xfeff; / &#xfeff;</span><span class="geo-nondefault"><span class="vcard"><span class="geo-dec" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location">10.300°N 109.217°W</span><span style="display:none">&#xfeff; / <span class="geo">10.300; -109.217</span></span><span style="display:none">&#xfeff; (<span class="fn org">Clipperton Island</span>)</span></span></span></a></span>. Clipperton is about 945&#160;<a href="/wiki/Kilometre" title="Kilometre">km</a> (510&#160;<a href="/wiki/Nautical_mile" title="Nautical mile">nmi</a>) south-east of <a href="/wiki/Socorro_Island" title="Socorro Island">Socorro Island</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Revillagigedo_Islands" title="Revillagigedo Islands">Revillagigedo Archipelago</a>, which is the nearest land, while the nearest French-owned island is <a href="/wiki/Hiva_Oa" title="Hiva Oa">Hiva Oa</a>. Some consider it to be one of the eastern-most points of <a href="/wiki/Oceania" title="Oceania">Oceania</a>, rather than an outlying island of the <a href="/wiki/Americas" title="Americas">Americas</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-global_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-global-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>It is low-lying and largely barren, with some scattered grasses, and a few clumps of <a href="/wiki/Coconut" title="Coconut">coconut palms (<i>Cocos nucifera</i>)</a>. Land elevations average 2&#160;<a href="/wiki/Metre" title="Metre">m</a> (6.6&#160;<a href="/wiki/Foot_(unit)" title="Foot (unit)">ft</a>), though a small <a href="/wiki/Volcano" title="Volcano">volcanic</a> outcrop rising to 29&#160;<a href="/wiki/Metre" title="Metre">m</a> (95&#160;<a href="/wiki/Foot_(unit)" title="Foot (unit)">ft</a>) on its south-east side is considerably higher and is referred to as 'Clipperton Rock'.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> The surrounding reef is exposed at low tide.<sup id="cite_ref-ClippertonIsland_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ClippertonIsland-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> The presence of this rock means that technically Clipperton is not an <a href="/wiki/Atoll" title="Atoll">atoll</a> but an island with a barrier reef.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Clipperton has had no permanent inhabitants since 1945. It is visited on occasion by fishermen, <a href="/wiki/French_Navy" title="French Navy">French Navy</a> patrols, scientific researchers, film crews, and shipwreck survivors. It has become a popular site for transmissions by <a href="/wiki/Amateur_radio" title="Amateur radio">amateur radio</a> operators.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Environment">Environment</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Environment">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Lagoon_and_climate">Lagoon and climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Lagoon and climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Clipperton is a ring-shaped <a href="/wiki/Atoll" title="Atoll">atoll</a>, which completely encloses a <a href="/wiki/Water_stagnation" title="Water stagnation">stagnant</a> <a href="/wiki/Fresh_water" title="Fresh water">fresh water</a> lagoon, and is 12&#160;<a href="/wiki/Kilometre" title="Kilometre">km</a> (7.5&#160;<a href="/wiki/Mile" title="Mile">mi</a>) in circumference. The lagoon is devoid of fish, and contains some deep basins, with depths of 43 and 72&#160;<a href="/wiki/Metre" title="Metre">m</a> (141 and 236&#160;<a href="/wiki/Foot_(unit)" title="Foot (unit)">ft</a>), including a spot known as Trou-Sans-Fond, or 'the bottomless hole', with <a href="/wiki/Acid" title="Acid">acidic</a> water at its base. The water is described as being almost fresh at the surface, and highly <a href="/wiki/Eutrophication" title="Eutrophication">eutrophic</a>. Seaweed beds cover approximately 45&#160;percent of the lagoon's surface. The rim averages 150&#160;<a href="/wiki/Metre" title="Metre">m</a> (490&#160;<a href="/wiki/Foot_(unit)" title="Foot (unit)">ft</a>) in width, reaching 400&#160;<a href="/wiki/Metre" title="Metre">m</a> (1,300&#160;<a href="/wiki/Foot_(unit)" title="Foot (unit)">ft</a>) in the west, and narrows to 45&#160;<a href="/wiki/Metre" title="Metre">m</a> (148&#160;<a href="/wiki/Foot_(unit)" title="Foot (unit)">ft</a>) in the north-east, where sea waves occasionally spill over into the lagoon.<sup id="cite_ref-ClippertonIsland_14-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ClippertonIsland-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>While some sources have rated the lagoon water as non-<a href="/wiki/Drinking_water" title="Drinking water">potable</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> testimony from the crew of the tuna clipper M/V <i>Monarch,</i> stranded for 23&#160;days in 1962 after their boat sank, indicates otherwise. Their report reveals that the lagoon water, while not tasting very good, was drinkable, though "muddy and dirty". Several of the castaways drank it, with no apparent ill effects.<sup id="cite_ref-No94_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-No94-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Survivors of an ill-fated Mexican military colony in 1917 (see below) indicated that they were dependent upon rain for their water supply, catching it in old boats they used for this purpose.<sup id="cite_ref-No94_18-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-No94-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> Aside from the lagoon and water caught from rain, no other freshwater sources are known to exist.
</p><p>It has a tropical oceanic climate, with average temperatures of 20–32&#160;<a href="/wiki/Celsius" title="Celsius">°C</a> (68–90&#160;<a href="/wiki/Fahrenheit" title="Fahrenheit">°F</a>). The rainy season occurs from May to October, when it is subject to tropical storms and hurricanes. Surrounding ocean waters are warm, pushed by equatorial and counter-equatorial currents. It has no known natural resources (its <a href="/wiki/Guano" title="Guano">guano</a> having been depleted early in the 20th&#160;century). Although 115&#160;species of fish have been identified in nearby waters, the only economic activity in the area is <a href="/wiki/Tuna" title="Tuna">tuna</a> fishing.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Flora_and_fauna">Flora and fauna</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Flora and fauna">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Clippertonisland.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/12/Clippertonisland.jpg/220px-Clippertonisland.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="163" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/12/Clippertonisland.jpg/330px-Clippertonisland.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/12/Clippertonisland.jpg/440px-Clippertonisland.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1574" data-file-height="1164" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Clippertonisland.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Coconut" title="Coconut">Coconut</a> palms on Clipperton. The lagoon is visible beyond the trees</div></div></div>
<p>When Snodgrass and Heller visited in 1898, they reported that "no land plant is native to the island".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESnodgrassHeller1902_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESnodgrassHeller1902-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> Historical accounts from 1711, 1825, and 1839 show a low grassy or suffrutescent (partially woody) flora. During Sachet's visit in 1958, the vegetation was found to consist of a sparse cover of spiny grass and low thickets, a creeping plant (<i><a href="/wiki/Ipomoea" title="Ipomoea">Ipomoea</a></i> spp.), and stands of coconut palm. This low-lying herbaceous flora seems to be a pioneer in nature, and most of it is believed to be composed of recently introduced species. Sachet suspected that <i><a href="/wiki/Heliotropium_curassavicum" title="Heliotropium curassavicum">Heliotropium curassavicum</a></i>, and possibly <i><a href="/wiki/Portulaca_oleracea" title="Portulaca oleracea">Portulaca oleracea</a></i> were native. <a href="/wiki/Coconut" title="Coconut">Coconut</a> palms and <a href="/wiki/Pig" title="Pig">pigs</a> were introduced in the 1890s by <a href="/wiki/Guano" title="Guano">guano</a> miners. The pigs reduced the crab population, which in turn allowed <a href="/wiki/Grassland" title="Grassland">grassland</a> to gradually cover about 80&#160;percent of the land surface.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> The elimination of these pigs in 1958, the result of a personal project by <a href="/wiki/Kenneth_E._Stager" title="Kenneth E. Stager">Kenneth E. Stager</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-Pitman_et_al._2005_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pitman_et_al._2005-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> has caused most of this vegetation to disappear, as the population of land crabs (<i><a href="/wiki/Johngarthia_planata" title="Johngarthia planata">Johngarthia planata</a></i>) has returned to millions.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> The result is virtually a sandy desert, with only 674&#160;palms counted by <a href="/wiki/Christian_Jost_(geographer)" title="Christian Jost (geographer)">Christian Jost</a> during the 'Passion 2001' French mission, and five islets in the lagoon with grass that the terrestrial crabs cannot reach.
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Jielbeaumadier_crabe_de_clipperton_mjp_paris_2014.jpeg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/51/Jielbeaumadier_crabe_de_clipperton_mjp_paris_2014.jpeg/220px-Jielbeaumadier_crabe_de_clipperton_mjp_paris_2014.jpeg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/51/Jielbeaumadier_crabe_de_clipperton_mjp_paris_2014.jpeg/330px-Jielbeaumadier_crabe_de_clipperton_mjp_paris_2014.jpeg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/51/Jielbeaumadier_crabe_de_clipperton_mjp_paris_2014.jpeg/440px-Jielbeaumadier_crabe_de_clipperton_mjp_paris_2014.jpeg 2x" data-file-width="4000" data-file-height="3000" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Jielbeaumadier_crabe_de_clipperton_mjp_paris_2014.jpeg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A bright-orange Clipperton crab (<i><a href="/wiki/Johngarthia_planata" title="Johngarthia planata">Johngarthia planata</a></i>)</div></div></div>
<p>On the north-west side, the most abundant plant species are <i><a href="/wiki/Cenchrus_echinatus" title="Cenchrus echinatus">Cenchrus echinatus</a></i>, <i><a href="/wiki/Sida_rhombifolia" title="Sida rhombifolia">Sida rhombifolia</a></i>, and <i>Corchorus aestuans</i>. These plants compose a shrub cover up to 30&#160;<a href="/wiki/Centimetre" title="Centimetre">cm</a> (12&#160;<a href="/wiki/Inch" title="Inch">in</a>) in height, and are intermixed with <i><a href="/wiki/Eclipta_(plant)" title="Eclipta (plant)">Eclipta</a></i>, <i><a href="/wiki/Phyllanthus" title="Phyllanthus">Phyllanthus</a></i>, and <i><a href="/wiki/Solanum" title="Solanum">Solanum</a></i>, as well as a taller plant, <i><a href="/wiki/Brassica_juncea" title="Brassica juncea">Brassica juncea</a></i>. A unique feature is that the vegetation is arranged in parallel rows of species, with dense rows of taller species alternating with lower, more open vegetation. This was assumed to be a result of the <a href="/wiki/Phosphate" title="Phosphate">phosphate</a> mining method of trench-digging.<sup id="cite_ref-ClippertonIsland_14-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ClippertonIsland-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Brown_booby.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/Brown_booby.jpg/220px-Brown_booby.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="181" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/Brown_booby.jpg/330px-Brown_booby.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/Brown_booby.jpg/440px-Brown_booby.jpg 2x" data-file-width="498" data-file-height="410" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Brown_booby.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A <a href="/wiki/Brown_booby" title="Brown booby">brown booby</a></div></div></div>
<p>The only land animals known to exist are two species of reptiles (<i><a href="/wiki/Gehyra_insulensis" class="mw-redirect" title="Gehyra insulensis">Gehyra insulensis</a></i>, a gecko, and <i><a href="/wiki/Emoia_cyanura" title="Emoia cyanura">Emoia cyanura</a>,</i> a skink),<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> bright-orange land crabs (<i><a href="/wiki/Johngarthia_planata" title="Johngarthia planata">Johngarthia planata</a></i>, sometimes known as the 'Clipperton Crab', although it is also found on other islands in the eastern Pacific), birds, and rats. The rats probably arrived on large fishing boats that were wrecked on the island in 1999 and 2000.<sup id="cite_ref-Pitman_et_al._2005_21-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pitman_et_al._2005-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> Bird species include <a href="/wiki/White_tern" title="White tern">white terns</a>, <a href="/wiki/Masked_booby" title="Masked booby">masked boobies</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sooty_tern" title="Sooty tern">sooty terns</a>, <a href="/wiki/Brown_booby" title="Brown booby">brown boobies</a>, <a href="/wiki/Brown_noddy" title="Brown noddy">brown noddies</a>, <a href="/wiki/Black_noddy" title="Black noddy">black noddies</a>, <a href="/wiki/Greater_frigatebird" class="mw-redirect" title="Greater frigatebird">great frigatebirds</a>, <a href="/wiki/Coot" title="Coot">coots</a>, <a href="/wiki/Swallow" title="Swallow">martins</a> (swallows), <a href="/wiki/Cuckoo" title="Cuckoo">cuckoos</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Yellow_warbler" class="mw-redirect" title="Yellow warbler">yellow warblers</a>. Ducks have been reported in the lagoon.<sup id="cite_ref-ClippertonIsland_14-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ClippertonIsland-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> The island has been identified as an <a href="/wiki/Important_Bird_Area" title="Important Bird Area">Important Bird Area</a> by <a href="/wiki/BirdLife_International" title="BirdLife International">BirdLife International</a> because of the large breeding colony of masked boobies, with 110,000&#160;individual birds recorded.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> The lagoon harbours millions of <a href="/wiki/Isopod" class="mw-redirect" title="Isopod">isopods</a>, which are said to deliver an especially painful sting.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A 2005 report by the <a href="/wiki/National_Oceanic_and_Atmospheric_Administration" title="National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration">National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administrations</a> (NOAA) Southwest Fisheries Science Center indicated that the increased rat presence had led to a decline in both crab and bird populations, causing a corresponding increase in both vegetation and coconut palms. This report urgently recommended eradication of rats, so that vegetation might be reduced, and the island might return to its 'pre-human' state.<sup id="cite_ref-Pitman_et_al._2005_21-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pitman_et_al._2005-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Discovery_and_early_claims">Discovery and early claims</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Discovery and early claims">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:$1_stamp_of_Clipperton_Island.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/%241_stamp_of_Clipperton_Island.jpg/220px-%241_stamp_of_Clipperton_Island.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="241" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/0/02/%241_stamp_of_Clipperton_Island.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="325" data-file-height="356" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:$1_stamp_of_Clipperton_Island.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>1895 $1 stamp of Clipperton Island, issued as part of the U.S. claim to the island</div></div></div>
<p>The island is believed to have been discovered by <a href="/wiki/Spaniards" title="Spaniards">Spaniard</a> <a href="/wiki/%C3%81lvaro_de_Saavedra_Cer%C3%B3n" title="Álvaro de Saavedra Cerón">Alvaro Saavedra Cerón</a> on 15 November 1528.<sup id="cite_ref-Vargas,_2011_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Vargas,_2011-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> The expedition was commissioned by <a href="/wiki/Hern%C3%A1n_Cort%C3%A9s" title="Hernán Cortés">Hernán Cortés</a>, the Spanish Conquistador in Mexico, to find a route to the <a href="/wiki/Philippines" title="Philippines">Philippines</a>. Others claim that Portuguese-born Spanish explorer <a href="/wiki/Ferdinand_Magellan" title="Ferdinand Magellan">Ferdinand Magellan</a> was the first to find it in 1521,<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> which would make Clipperton and certain islands of <a href="/wiki/Micronesia" title="Micronesia">Micronesia</a> the first areas of the Pacific to be reached by Europeans.<sup id="cite_ref-global_7-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-global-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The island was rediscovered on <a href="/wiki/Good_Friday" title="Good Friday">Good Friday</a>, 3&#160;April 1711, by Frenchmen <a href="/w/index.php?title=Martin_de_Chassiron&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Martin de Chassiron (page does not exist)">Martin de Chassiron</a> and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Michel_Du_Bocage&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Michel Du Bocage (page does not exist)">Michel Du Bocage</a>, commanding the French ships <i>La Princesse</i> and <i>La Découverte</i>. It was given the name <i>Île de la Passion</i> (<i>Passion Island</i>); the date of rediscovery fell within <a href="/wiki/Passiontide" title="Passiontide">Passiontide</a>. They drew up the first map, and claimed the island for France. The first scientific expedition took place in 1725, under Frenchman M. Bocage, who lived on the island for several months.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> In 1858, France formally laid claim.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The current name comes from <a href="/wiki/John_Clipperton" title="John Clipperton">John Clipperton</a>, an English <a href="/wiki/Pirate" class="mw-redirect" title="Pirate">pirate</a> and <a href="/wiki/Privateer" title="Privateer">privateer</a> who fought the Spanish during the early 18th century, and who is said to have passed by the island. Some sources claim that he used it as a base for his raids on shipping.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Other claimants included the United States, whose American Guano Mining Company claimed it under the <a href="/wiki/Guano_Islands_Act" title="Guano Islands Act">Guano Islands Act</a> of 1856; Mexico also claimed it due to activities undertaken there as early as 1848–1849. On 17&#160;November 1858 Emperor <a href="/wiki/Napoleon_III_of_France" class="mw-redirect" title="Napoleon III of France">Napoleon&#160;III</a> annexed it as part of the French colony of <a href="/wiki/Tahiti" title="Tahiti">Tahiti</a>. This did not settle the ownership question. On 24&#160;November 1897, French naval authorities found three Americans working for the American Guano Company, who had raised the <a href="/wiki/American_flag" class="mw-redirect" title="American flag">American flag</a>. U.S. authorities denounced their act, assuring the French that they did not intend to assert American sovereignty.<sup id="cite_ref-AJIL26_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AJIL26-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> Mexico reasserted its claim late in the 19th century, and on 13&#160;December 1897 sent the gunboat <i>La Demócrata</i> to occupy and annex it. A colony was established, and a series of military governors was posted, the last one being <a href="/wiki/Ram%C3%B3n_Arnaud" title="Ramón Arnaud">Ramón Arnaud</a> (1906–1916).
</p>
<h3><span id="Guano_mining.2C_Mexican_colony.2C_and_evacuation_of_1917"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Guano_mining,_Mexican_colony,_and_evacuation_of_1917">Guano mining, Mexican colony, and evacuation of 1917</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Guano mining, Mexican colony, and evacuation of 1917">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:SobrevivientesClipperton.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2e/SobrevivientesClipperton.jpg/220px-SobrevivientesClipperton.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="131" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2e/SobrevivientesClipperton.jpg/330px-SobrevivientesClipperton.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2e/SobrevivientesClipperton.jpg/440px-SobrevivientesClipperton.jpg 2x" data-file-width="786" data-file-height="467" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:SobrevivientesClipperton.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Mexican survivors from Clipperton Island, 1917</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/w/index.php?title=British_Pacific_Island_Company&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="British Pacific Island Company (page does not exist)">British Pacific Island Company</a> acquired the rights to guano deposits in 1906, and built a mining settlement in conjunction with the Mexican government. That same year, a <a href="/wiki/Lighthouse" title="Lighthouse">lighthouse</a> was erected, under the orders of <a href="/wiki/List_of_Presidents_of_Mexico" class="mw-redirect" title="List of Presidents of Mexico">President</a> <a href="/wiki/Porfirio_D%C3%ADaz" title="Porfirio Díaz">Porfirio Díaz</a>. By 1914, around 100 people; men, women, and children, were living there, resupplied every two months by a ship from <a href="/wiki/Acapulco" title="Acapulco">Acapulco</a>. With the escalation of fighting in the <a href="/wiki/Mexican_Revolution" title="Mexican Revolution">Mexican Revolution</a>, the regular resupply visits ceased, and the inhabitants were left to their own devices.<sup id="cite_ref-ClippertonProject_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ClippertonProject-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>By 1917, all but one of the male inhabitants had died. Many had perished from <a href="/wiki/Scurvy" title="Scurvy">scurvy</a>, while others (including Captain Arnaud) died during an attempt to sail after a passing ship to fetch help. Lighthouse keeper Victoriano Álvarez was the last man on the island, together with 15 women and children.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> Álvarez proclaimed himself 'king', and began a campaign of rape and murder, before being killed by Tirza Rendón, who was his favourite victim. Almost immediately after Álvarez's death, four women and seven children, the last survivors, were picked up by the US Navy gunship <a href="/wiki/USS_Yorktown_(PG-1)" title="USS Yorktown (PG-1)"><i>Yorktown</i></a> on 18 July 1917.<sup id="cite_ref-ClippertonProject_35-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ClippertonProject-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> No more attempts were made to colonise it, though it was briefly occupied during the 1930s and 1940s.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The story of the Mexican colony has been the subject of several novels, including Ivo Mansmann's <i>Clipperton, Schicksale auf einer vergessenen Insel</i> ('Clipperton, Destinies on a Forgotten Island') in German,<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> Colombian writer <a href="/wiki/Laura_Restrepo" title="Laura Restrepo">Laura Restrepo</a>'s <i>La Isla de la Pasión</i> ('Passion Island') in Spanish,<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> and Ana García Bergua's <i>Isla de Bobos</i> ('Island of Fools'), also in Spanish.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The history of the island and of Captain <a href="/wiki/Ram%C3%B3n_Arnaud" title="Ramón Arnaud">Ramón Arnaud</a> was written by his niece Gabriela Arnaud; <i>Clipperton, Una Historia de Honor y Gloria</i> ('Clipperton, A History of Honour and Glory') in Spanish.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Final_arbitration_of_ownership">Final arbitration of ownership</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Final arbitration of ownership">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>France insisted on its ownership, and a lengthy diplomatic correspondence between Mexico and France led to the conclusion of a treaty on 2 March 1909, to seek binding <a href="/wiki/International_arbitration" title="International arbitration">international arbitration</a> by King <a href="/wiki/Victor_Emmanuel_III_of_Italy" title="Victor Emmanuel III of Italy">Victor Emmanuel&#160;III of Italy</a>, with each nation promising to abide by his determination.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1931, King Victor Emmanuel III issued his arbitral decision in the <i>Clipperton Island Case</i>, declaring Clipperton to be a French possession.<sup id="cite_ref-AJIL26_33-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AJIL26-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Post-World_War_II_developments">Post-World War II developments</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Post-World War II developments">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The island was abandoned by the end of <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, after being briefly occupied by the US from 1944 to 1945. Since then, it has been visited by sports fishermen, patrols of the French Navy, and by Mexican tuna and shark fishermen. There have been infrequent scientific and amateur radio expeditions, and in 1978 <a href="/wiki/Jacques-Yves_Cousteau" class="mw-redirect" title="Jacques-Yves Cousteau">Jacques-Yves Cousteau</a> visited with his team of divers and a survivor from the 1917 evacuation, to film a television special called <i>Clipperton: The Island that Time Forgot</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>It was visited by ornithologist <a href="/wiki/Kenneth_E._Stager" title="Kenneth E. Stager">Ken Stager</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Natural_History_Museum_of_Los_Angeles_County" title="Natural History Museum of Los Angeles County">Los Angeles County Museum</a> in 1958. Appalled at the depredations visited by feral pigs upon the island's <a href="/wiki/Brown_booby" title="Brown booby">brown booby</a> and <a href="/wiki/Masked_booby" title="Masked booby">masked booby</a> colonies (reduced to 500 and 150&#160;birds, respectively), Stager procured a shotgun and killed all 58&#160;pigs. By 2003, the booby colonies had 25,000&#160;brown boobies and 112,000&#160;masked boobies, the world's second-largest brown booby colony, and its largest masked booby colony.<sup id="cite_ref-Pitman_et_al._2005_21-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pitman_et_al._2005-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> In 1994, this story inspired Bernie Tershy and Don Croll, both Professors at <a href="/wiki/University_of_California,_Santa_Cruz" title="University of California, Santa Cruz">UCSC's</a> <a href="/wiki/Long_Marine_Laboratory" class="mw-redirect" title="Long Marine Laboratory">Long Marine Lab</a> to found the non-profit <a href="/wiki/Island_Conservation" title="Island Conservation">Island Conservation</a>  with the mission to prevent extinctions by removing invasive species from islands. 
</p><p>When the <a href="/wiki/Independence_of_Algeria" class="mw-redirect" title="Independence of Algeria">independence of Algeria</a> in 1962 threatened <a href="/wiki/French_nuclear_testing" class="mw-redirect" title="French nuclear testing">French nuclear testing</a> sites in Algeria, the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Defence_(France)" class="mw-redirect" title="Ministry of Defence (France)">French Ministry of Defence</a> considered Clipperton Island as a possible replacement. This was eventually ruled out, due to the island's hostile climate and remote location. The French explored reopening the lagoon and developing a harbour for trade and tourism during the 1970s, but this too was abandoned. An automatic weather installation was completed on 7&#160;April 1980, with data collected by this station transmitted directly by satellite to <a href="/wiki/Brittany" title="Brittany">Brittany</a>.
</p><p>In 1981, the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Academy_of_Sciences_for_Overseas_Territories&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Academy of Sciences for Overseas Territories (page does not exist)">Academy of Sciences for Overseas Territories</a> recommended that the island have its own economic infrastructure, with an <a href="/wiki/Airstrip" class="mw-redirect" title="Airstrip">airstrip</a> and a fishing port in the lagoon. This would mean opening up the lagoon by creating a passage in the atoll rim. For this purpose, an agreement was signed with the French government, represented by the High Commissioner for French Polynesia, whereby the island became French state property. In 1986, a meeting took place regarding the establishment of a permanent base for fishing, between the High Commissioner, and the survey firm for the development and exploitation of the island (<a href="/w/index.php?title=SEDEIC&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="SEDEIC (page does not exist)">SEDEIC</a>). Taking into account the economic constraints, the distance from markets, and the small size of the atoll, nothing apart from preliminary studies was undertaken. All plans for development were abandoned. In the mid-1980s, the French government also began efforts to enlist citizens of French Polynesia to settle on Clipperton; these plans were ultimately abandoned as well.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Castaways">Castaways</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Castaways">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In early 1962, the island provided a home to nine crewmen of the sunken tuna clipper MV <i>Monarch</i>, stranded for 23 days from 6 February to 1 March. They reported that the lagoon water was drinkable, though they preferred to drink water from the coconuts they found. Unable to use any of the dilapidated buildings, they constructed a crude shelter from cement bags and tin salvaged from <a href="/wiki/Quonset_hut" title="Quonset hut">Quonset huts</a> built by the American military 20 years earlier. Wood from the huts was used for firewood, and fish caught off the fringing reef combined with some potatoes and onions they had saved from their sinking vessel augmented the island's meager supply of coconuts. The crewmen reported that they tried eating bird's eggs, but found them to be rancid, and they decided after trying to cook a 'little black bird' that it did not have enough meat to make the effort worthwhile. Pigs had been eradicated, though the crewmen reported seeing their skeletons around the atoll. The crewmen were eventually discovered by another fishing boat, and rescued by the United States Navy destroyer <a href="/wiki/USS_Robison_(DDG-12)" class="mw-redirect" title="USS Robison (DDG-12)">USS <i>Robison</i></a>.<sup id="cite_ref-No94_18-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-No94-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1988, five Mexican fishermen became lost at sea after a storm during their trip along the coast of <a href="/wiki/Costa_Rica" title="Costa Rica">Costa Rica</a>. They drifted within sight of the island, but were unable to reach it.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> Steven Longbaugh and David Heritage, two American deckhands from a <a href="/wiki/Fishing_boat" class="mw-redirect" title="Fishing boat">fishing boat</a> based in California, were stranded for three weeks in 1998. They were rescued after rebuilding a survival radio, and using distress flares to signal for help.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="The material near this tag needs to be fact-checked with the cited source(s). (January 2018)">verification needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="21st_century">21st century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: 21st century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Clipperton_Waves.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e4/Clipperton_Waves.jpg/220px-Clipperton_Waves.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="131" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e4/Clipperton_Waves.jpg/330px-Clipperton_Waves.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e4/Clipperton_Waves.jpg/440px-Clipperton_Waves.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1426" data-file-height="847" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Clipperton_Waves.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Surf on Clipperton Island</div></div></div>
<p>The Mexican and French oceanographic expedition <a href="/w/index.php?title=SURPACLIP&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="SURPACLIP (page does not exist)">SURPACLIP</a> (<a href="/w/index.php?title=UNAM_Mexico&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="UNAM Mexico (page does not exist)">UNAM Mexico</a> and <a href="/w/index.php?title=UNC_Noum%C3%A9a&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="UNC Nouméa (page does not exist)">UNC Nouméa</a>) made extensive studies in 1997. In 2001, French geographer <a href="/wiki/Christian_Jost_(geographer)" title="Christian Jost (geographer)">Christian Jost</a> extended the 1997 studies through his French 'Passion 2001' expedition, explaining the evolution of the ecosystem, and releasing several papers, a video film, and a website.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> In 2003, Lance Milbrand<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> stayed for 41&#160;days on a <a href="/wiki/National_Geographic_Society" title="National Geographic Society">National Geographic Society</a> expedition, recording his adventure in video, photos, and a written diary (see links below).
</p><p>In 2005, the <a href="/wiki/Ecosystem" title="Ecosystem">ecosystem</a> was extensively studied for four months by a scientific mission organised by <a href="/wiki/Jean-Louis_%C3%89tienne" title="Jean-Louis Étienne">Jean-Louis Étienne</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> which made a complete inventory of mineral, plant, and animal species, studied algae as deep as 100&#160;<a href="/wiki/Metre" title="Metre">m</a> (328&#160;<a href="/wiki/Foot_(unit)" title="Foot (unit)">ft</a>) below sea level, and examined the effects of pollution. A 2008 expedition from the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Washington" title="University of Washington">University of Washington</a>'s School of Oceanography collected sediment cores from the lagoon, to study climate change over the last millennium.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 21 February 2007, administration was transferred from the <a href="/wiki/High_commissioner" title="High commissioner">High Commissioner</a> of the Republic in <a href="/wiki/French_Polynesia" title="French Polynesia">French Polynesia</a> to the Minister of Overseas France.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2007, a recreational scuba diving expedition explored the reefs surrounding Clipperton, and compared the marine life with the reports of the Connie Limbaugh (Scripps) expeditions in 1956 and 1958. Recreational scuba diving expeditions are now made every spring.
</p><p>During the night of 10&#160;February 2010, the <i>Sichem Osprey</i>, a Maltese <a href="/wiki/Chemical_tanker" title="Chemical tanker">chemical tanker</a>, ran aground en route from the <a href="/wiki/Panama_Canal" title="Panama Canal">Panama Canal</a> to <a href="/wiki/South_Korea" title="South Korea">South Korea</a>. The 170&#160;<a href="/wiki/Metre" title="Metre">m</a> (558&#160;<a href="/wiki/Foot_(unit)" title="Foot (unit)">ft</a>) ship contained <a href="/wiki/Xylene" title="Xylene">xylene</a>, a clear, flammable volatile liquid. All 19&#160;crew members were reported safe, and the vessel reported no leaks.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> The vessel was re-floated on 6&#160;March<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> and returned to service.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In mid-March 2012, the crew from the Clipperton Project<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> noted the widespread presence of refuse, particularly on the north-east shore, and around the Rock. Debris, including plastic bottles and containers, create a potentially harmful environment for its flora and fauna. This trash is common to only two beaches (North East and South West), and the rest of the island is fairly clean. Other refuse has been left after occupation by Americans 1944–1945, French 1966–1969, and the 2008 scientific expedition.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Amateur_radio_DX-peditions">Amateur radio DX-peditions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Amateur radio DX-peditions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The island has long been an attractive destination for <a href="/wiki/Amateur_radio" title="Amateur radio">amateur radio</a> groups, due to its remoteness, the difficulty of landing, permit requirements, garish history, and interesting environment. While some radio operation was done ancillary to other expeditions, major DX-peditions include FO0XB (1978), FO0XX (1985), FO0CI (1992), FO0AAA (2000), and TX5C (2008).
</p><p>One <a href="/wiki/DX-pedition" title="DX-pedition">DX-pedition</a> was the Cordell Expedition in March&#160;2013 using the callsign TX5K,<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> organised and led by <a href="/wiki/Robert_Schmieder" title="Robert Schmieder">Robert Schmieder</a>. The project combined radio operations with selected scientific investigations. The team of 24&#160;radio operators made more than 114,000&#160;contacts, breaking the previous record of 75,000. The activity included extensive operation of the <a href="/wiki/6-meter_band" title="6-meter band">6 meter band</a>, including <a href="/wiki/Earth%E2%80%93Moon%E2%80%93Earth_communication" title="Earth–Moon–Earth communication">Earth–Moon–Earth communication</a> (EME) or 'moonbounce' contacts. A notable accomplishment was the use of DXA, a real-time satellite-based online graphic radio log web page, that allowed anyone anywhere with a browser to see the radio activity. Scientific work carried out during the expedition included the first collection and identification of <a href="/wiki/Foraminifera" title="Foraminifera">foraminifera</a>, and extensive aerial imaging of the island using kite-borne cameras. The team included two scientists from the French-Polynesian <a href="/wiki/University_of_Tahiti" class="mw-redirect" title="University of Tahiti">University of Tahiti</a>, and a TV crew from the French documentary television series <a href="/wiki/Thalassa_(TV_series)" title="Thalassa (TV series)">Thalassa</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>An April&#160;2015 DX-pedition using callsign TX5P was conducted by Alain Duchauchoy, F6BFH, concurrent with the Passion 2015 scientific expedition to Clipperton Island, and engaging in research of Mexican use of the island during the early 1900s.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Postal_code">Postal code</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Postal code">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The island is assigned with <a href="/wiki/Postal_codes_in_France" title="Postal codes in France">French postal code</a> 98799. However, there is no <a href="/wiki/Post_office" title="Post office">post office</a> on the island.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
